### [2026-09-05T05:30:57+00:00] Phase 0 Step 0.2 — field census (before)

Command: `.venv-corpus/bin/python scripts/revamp/field_census.py`
Script: `scripts/revamp/field_census.py` (flags `--fields-dir`, `--assignment`, `--out`, `--out-json`; idempotent — byte-identical outputs on a second run, so it re-runs unchanged after Phase 2)
Output: `data/revamp/field-census-before.csv`, `data/revamp/field-census-before.json`

30 distinct field names across the three models over all 28,832 pages (every assignment key has a field
record; none missing, none extra). Tiers derived by the promotion rule: Tier 1 1,719 · Tier 2 4,477 ·
Tier 3 22,636. Per-tier `pages` is the count of pages in that tier carrying the field at all, so
present + absent + not-applicable equals it; Tier 1 splits into 1,109 LONGEVITY-model pages and 610
withdrawn CLINICAL-model pages, which is why the clinical-only fields show a Tier 1 denominator of 610
and the four shared fields (faers, interactions, regulatory, trialFailures) show 1,719.

Universally blank fields (present on under 1% of pages in every tier where the field appears at all):
**patentStatus** — the only one. It appears on Tier 3 alone (22,636 pages: 0 present, 35 absent,
22,601 not-applicable, recorded as "the Orange Book lists patents only against an approved US
application"). No Tier 1 or Tier 2 page carries a patent field at all, so patent status is absent from
the schema for 6,196 pages rather than blank on them. Nothing else falls under 1% in any tier; the next
lowest are itp (4.6% of Tier 1), withdrawal (4.4% of Tier 2), whyStopped (8.0% of Tier 3), clocks (8.7%
of Tier 1) and relatedOnTarget (9.6% of Tier 3).

The full 30-row table with per-tier and total counts is in the CSV; the JSON carries the same rows plus
`tierSizes`, the tier rule text and the input paths.

### [2026-09-05T07:15:38+00:00] Phase 1 Step 1.3 — re-derive the threshold per tier over applicable fields

Command: `.venv-corpus/bin/python scripts/revamp/derive_threshold.py`
Script: `scripts/revamp/derive_threshold.py` (flags `--census`, `--refresh`, `--applicability-only`; idempotent — it recomputes from the field batches and the current census on every run and reproduced identical thresholds on a second run, so Phase 2 re-runs it unchanged)
Output: `data/revamp/presence-applicable.ndjson`, `data/revamp/field-census-applicable.csv`, `data/revamp/thresholds.json`, `data/revamp/thresholds.md` (fold scores cached under `data/revamp/overlap/`)

Result, five lines:
1. Tier 1 threshold **7** → 993 indexable; positional 0.163482 all pairs and 0.134359 size-matched, lexical 0.324224 size-matched; both lines clear on the rule's own answer, so no step was needed.
2. Tier 2's bucket rule answers **8** (987 pages, positional 0.138846) but no count from 8 to its ceiling of 11 brings the set's size-matched lexical median under 0.353 (0.412005, 0.405629, 0.402659, 0.408602) — Tier 2 has no threshold that holds both lines and 0 indexable pages; the blocker is shared vocabulary, not field count.
3. Tier 3's bucket rule selects nothing: its best bucket positional median is 0.2982 at count 7, so no count is eligible and no page is indexable — consistent with Tier 3 carrying `noindex`.
4. Method: gate1b_v3's rule verbatim over the present-over-applicable count on the current (v4) render text; population is Gate 1b's own (pages with ≥ 1 question) with the tier restriction lifted, because per-tier folds pack same-model pages and lift every median (Tier 1 best 0.2340, Tier 2 0.2667) while whole-corpus folds dilute them (Tier 1 answers 6) — both are recorded under `basisSensitivity` and neither selects; positional is read all-pairs, lexical on the set's own size-matched folds of 324, the basis 0.353 was measured on.
5. Stub rule: a count below R15's stub floor of 3 cannot be selected, so every page in an eligible bucket holds ≥ 3 applicable fields and a 1-of-1 page can never read as fully covered; 3,512 Tier 3 pages hold fewer than 3 applicable fields and are reported separately; `patentStatus` stays out as `pending_source` and 132 Tier 2 `withdrawal` entries recorded present with `withdrawn: false` leave both numerator and denominator.

### [2026-09-05T07:15:38+00:00] Phase 1 Step 1.4 — explain Tier 2's 26 of 4,477

Command: `.venv-corpus/bin/python scripts/revamp/tier2_anomaly.py`
Script: `scripts/revamp/tier2_anomaly.py`
Output: `data/revamp/tier2-anomaly.csv`

Result, five lines:
1. **Both, and the schema half is the larger one.** The recorded 26 is reproduced exactly from `page-meta-v4.json` and was arithmetic: 11 is the CLINICAL model's exact ceiling, so a Tier 2 page cleared it only by holding all 11 of its fields. Under the corrected count the same threshold of 11 passes 5 pages; at Tier 2's own bucket-rule answer of 8 it passes **987 of 4,477 (22.0 %)**, a 38-fold rise over 26 — the sixtyfold Tier 1/Tier 2 gap was a schema artefact.
2. Schema artefact only: `withdrawal` — 4,412 of 4,477 Tier 2 pages are not withdrawn so it does not apply; on the 65 that are it is present on 100 % against a recorded 4.40 %.
3. Both: `indication` 52.89 % → 61.05 %, `labelKinetics` 27.00 % → 31.17 %, `adverseEvents` 31.38 % → 36.22 %, `interactions` 15.08 % → 17.40 % — each loses 598 pages to NA-LABEL-CLASS and each is still absent on over a thousand pages where it does apply (1,511 / 2,670 / 2,474 / 3,204).
4. Data fact only, denominator unchanged: `faers` 36.36 % (2,849 pages short), `trialFailures` 38.08 % (2,772), `doseStudied` 24.20 % (3,376), `approvalDate` 51.32 % (2,168), `trialHistory` 70.74 % (1,310), `regulatory` 75.18 % (1,111).
5. Bucket rule used, stated so it can be checked: data fact = absent on more than 1,000 applicable Tier 2 pages; schema artefact = the correction removes the field from more than 5 % of Tier 2 pages or drops recorded present entries. Phase 2's label, Inxight, DrugCentral and register sources address every field in both lists; none is closed by the ruler alone.

### [2026-09-05T07:15:38+00:00] Phase 1 Step 1.5 — the lead's reading (Fable)
Read: all 180 pages of `data/revamp/threshold-sanity.md` (51,637 words). Backlog of defects seen: `data/revamp/phase1-reading-backlog.md` (owned by Phases 3 and 4). Flagged for Felix: the file itself.

**Tier 1 (line at 7 of applicable).** At 7 a page carries a second independent evidence type beside its trial list — a register status with measured endpoints, an organism-ladder rung, or a label half-life — and that is the point at which a biohacker gets a picture rather than a citation. At 6 the sample is single-trial records and the seven near-identical pneumococcal serotype pages, which read as the same page seven times. At 8 the pages read well except one, a NaN-keyed identity merge that is a Phase 3 defect and not a threshold matter.

**Tier 2 (no threshold; the rule's own answer is 8).** At 8 and 9 the pages are useful — label indication, registers, phases, stop reasons, half-life — and would serve a reader deciding whether a medicine is worth a conversation. They are also visibly uniform, and the reading shows the cause of the lexical floor: dozens of repeated register rows dominate the text, so the shared vocabulary is partly rendering, not only content. At 7 the biosimilar pages are near-duplicates of each other and of their parents; the line is not what separates useful from not here, identity and rendering are.

**Tier 3 (stub floor of 3; no threshold).** At 3 a page is a registry record — sponsor, enrolment, phases, unposted results — which is an honest development record and not a reason to index. At 2 the pages are correctly stubs. At 4 the stop-reason line is the most informative thing on the tier (lotiglipron's transaminase termination, stannsoporfin's redefined population), and noindex is right for all of it.

Corrections applied to `docs/specs/field-applicability.md` after the script measured the records: the LONGEVITY denominator is 15 (16 with ITP), not 14; no Tier 3 page has exactly one applicable field (highestPhase applies to all), the floor is 2.

### [2026-09-05T15:58:35+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.1 — NCATS Inxight Drugs

Commands: `scripts/revamp/fetch_log.sh` (robots, terms, FRDB bulk zip); `inxight_fetch_stitcher.py --date 2026-09-05 --workers 4`; `inxight_map.py --date 2026-09-05`; `inxight_manifest.py --date 2026-09-05` (all under `scripts/revamp/`, run with `.venv-corpus/bin/python`).
Outputs: `data/sources/inxight/2026-09-05/` (47 files, 497,316,482 B, `manifest.json`, `legal/`, `requests.log` 21,178 requests); `data/sources/inxight/mapped.parquet`; `data/sources/inxight/coverage.json`; `data/sources/inxight/name-candidates-for-review.csv`; one appended row in `docs/data/LICENSES.md`.

Result: 227,146 rows on 17,857 pages — Tier 1 1,581/1,719 (91.97%), Tier 2 3,802/4,477 (84.92%), Tier 3 12,474/22,636 (55.11%) — gaining mechanism, targets, uses, marketingStatus (115 jurisdictions incl. Japan 3,467 and UK 1,765 pages), pk, adverseEvents and ddi (36,995 tier-B rows with the Inxight record id, 18,698 of them recording that no interaction was found).
Matched by UNII on 17,749 pages, InChIKey 3, skeleton 134 (linked `form_of`, never merged), name-candidate 268 pages from 167 unconfirmed names written to the Phase 3 review list; 265 source records unmatched.
The corpus `unii` field alone under-joins: a `K1:` key names a second substance form (metformin is keyed K1:9100L32L2N while its `unii` field holds 786Z46389E), so both are indexed — 3,001 extra API pulls that lifted Tier 3 from 51.24% to 55.11%.
DrugBank (1,024 values), Broad Institute Drug List (7,086) and the Pharmaceutical Manufacturing Encyclopedia (28,774) are dropped from every descriptive field on licence grounds, counted in `coverage.json`.
Licence: public domain, US Government work; evidence stored at `data/sources/inxight/2026-09-05/legal/disclaimer.html`, and neither drugs.ncats.io nor stitcher.ncats.io publishes a robots.txt (both HTTP 404, responses stored).

gsrs-dev-public.ncats.io mirror) → `.venv-corpus/bin/python scripts/revamp/gsrs_map.py --dump data/sources/gsrs/2026-09-05/raw/dump-public-2026-08-06.gsrs --date 2026-08-06` → `.venv-corpus/bin/python scripts/revamp/gsrs_manifest.py --date 2026-09-05`

### [2026-09-05T15:58:35+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.3 — PubChem

Legal first, before any data request: both `robots.txt` files disallow the routes this step needs — `pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov` disallows `/rest/pug/` for `User-agent: *`, and `ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov` disallows `/`. PubChem separately publishes terms that invite exactly this use, quoted verbatim in `manifest.json` under `legal_review`: the FTP README ("allows the user to download PubChem data in bulk … NCBI itself places no restrictions on the use or distribution of the data contained therein") and the programmatic-access USAGE POLICY ("not more than 5 requests per second … We cannot offer API keys"). Documents saved under `data/sources/pubchem/2026-09-05/legal/`; all 732 requests logged to `requests.log`.

Retrieved (`scripts/revamp/pubchem_fetch.py`, stages `bulk`, `unii`, `inchikey`, `cidprops`, `synonyms`): `CID-Identifiers.tsv.gz` (98 MB) and `CID-Synonym-filtered.gz` (969 MB, taken in eight parallel byte ranges after one connection settled at 45 KiB/s), both verified against the publisher's MD5; plus 354 cached PUG REST property responses covering 19,912 InChIKeys and 12,683 CIDs. `CID-InChI-Key.gz` was measured at 7.37 GB and left untaken under the 5 GB ceiling, its smaller equivalent being the 100-key InChIKey batch POST — recorded in `manifest.json` under `not_downloaded`. When the throttle header turned Black at about 2 requests/second, the remaining 8,500 single lookups moved onto the bulk synonym file, which is what PubChem's own documentation asks large jobs to do.

Mapped (`scripts/revamp/pubchem_map.py`, salts in `scripts/revamp/salts.txt`): 20,442 of 28,832 pages — Tier 1 1,328, Tier 2 2,667, Tier 3 16,447 — by UNII 10,691, full InChIKey 9,325, skeleton 235 linked `form_of` and never merged, name-candidate 191. 82,932 rows in `data/sources/pubchem/mapped.parquet`: `cid`, `mw` and `tpsa` on 19,907 pages, `xlogp` on 18,967, and `inchikey`, `smiles`, `connectivity_smiles` and `inchi` on the 557 pages that held no structure at all. Where PubChem holds several CIDs for one InChIKey the lowest is taken, so metformin resolves to CID 4091 rather than a duplicate record. Counts in `data/sources/pubchem/coverage.json`.

Honest limits: a property is only ever written from a record carrying the page's exact full InChIKey, so `corpusInchikeyAgreement` records 19,457 agreements and no disagreements by construction. 1,364 pages whose UNII PubChem maps to a different substance — beclomethasone against its dipropionate, cortisone against its acetate — gained no field and are listed in `data/revamp/pubchem-structure-conflicts.csv` for Phase 3. The 173 combination pages take a `component` relation, never a molecular weight. 191 name candidates await UNII or InChIKey confirmation in `data/revamp/pubchem-name-candidates.csv`; 8,390 pages reach no PubChem compound at all, most of them biologics, botanicals and mixtures, listed in `data/revamp/pubchem-unmatched-pages.csv`. PubChem's CID differs from the corpus-held CID on 2,636 pages.

### [2026-09-05T15:58:35+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.5 — DrugCentral

Legal first: `drugcentral.org` and `unmtid-dbs.net` serve no `robots.txt` (HTTP 404 on both, recorded in `data/sources/drugcentral/2026-09-05/legal/robots-check.json`); the licence page states CC BY-SA 4.0 (`legal/privacy-license.html`); the publisher offers bulk access itself, so no page was parsed for data. Every request is in `data/sources/drugcentral/2026-09-05/requests.log`.

Retrieved (`scripts/revamp/drugcentral_fetch.py`, `drugcentral_fetch_dump.py`, `drugcentral_export_tables.py`): the 1.40 GB PostgreSQL dump, size checked at 1,400,714,190 bytes before transfer and taken in six HTTP range segments after a single connection settled at 110 KiB/s; the fourteen tables named in the spec exported in full from the public read-only instance, which serves the identical release (dbversion 54, 2023-11-01, verified in `raw/tables/dbversion.csv`); the FDA/EMA/PMDA approval CSVs and the drug-target interaction TSV. 25 files, 1.45 GB, `manifest.json` carries a SHA256 and licence per file; `gzip -t` passes on the dump and `scripts/revamp/drugcentral_verify_dump_matches_tables.py` confirms all thirteen tables carry identical row counts in the dump and in the exports (`data/sources/drugcentral/dump-vs-tables-check.json`).

Mapped (`scripts/revamp/drugcentral_map.py`, salts in `scripts/revamp/salts.txt`): 4,995 structures onto 5,713 of 28,832 pages — Tier 1 1,226, Tier 2 2,419, Tier 3 2,068 — by UNII 4,888 pages, full InChIKey 219, skeleton 639 (linked `form_of`, never merged), name-candidate 148. 35,686 rows in `data/sources/drugcentral/mapped.parquet` across 16 fields including `regulatory.JP` on 751 pages, which closes Japan. Counts in `data/sources/drugcentral/coverage.json`.

Honest limits: 213 of 4,995 structures reach no page, and `coverage.json` lists exactly what they carry (73 indications, 120 contraindications, 23 FDA and 3 PMDA approvals, 18 mechanisms, 1,373 FAERS rows); 164 name candidates await UNII or InChIKey confirmation in `data/sources/drugcentral/name-candidates-for-review.json` for the Phase 3 review list; the release is 2023-11-01, so approvals after that date are absent, not negative. CC BY-SA 4.0 puts a share-alike obligation on any rendered page built from these fields — `docs/data/LICENSES.md` records it.

### [2026-09-05T20:52:47+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.10 — WITHDRAWN database (Charité): BLOCKED; withdrawal field filled from ChEMBL instead

- **Source blocked on licence, not availability.** `data/sources/withdrawn/2026-09-06/{manifest.json,requests.log,retry-evidence.txt,legal/}` (17 legal/evidence files, 124,400 bytes, SHA256 each; 0 data files, 0 data bytes). No licence, terms or copyright grant for the database contents is published on any page of either version; the only licence attaching to the work is CC BY-NC 4.0, the Open Access licence of both NAR papers. Operating rule 8 fails on both counts. Three retries at 2s/4s/8s against the bulk CSV and `index.php` returned HTTP 404; `cheminfo.charite.de` has no DNS A record; the group's live `tox.charite.de` host 404s on all three relocation candidates. Full evidence in `data/revamp/worklog-entries/2.10-blocker.md`.
- **Correction to the 2026-09-05 attempt, which recorded the Archive as holding nothing.** It holds the four v1 bulk SDF files (`withdrawn_all.sdf` 628,870 bytes, `withdrawn_withdrawn.sdf` 244,969, `withdrawn_discontinued.sdf` 324,993, `withdrawn_ema.sdf` 60,967) and ~500 v2 `drug_info.php` pages, indexed in `legal/cdx-cheminfo.txt` and `legal/cdx-withdrawn3.txt`. Deliberately **not** retrieved: the licence bar is independent of availability.
- **Second correction, and the substantive result.** The 2026-09-05 coverage recorded zero withdrawal reasons and zero years as available from any licensed source. That is false. ChEMBL 37 `drug_warning` (CC BY-SA 3.0, commercial use permitted, already on disk at `data/corpus-20k/raw/chembl/warning-*.json`) carries 1,014 `Withdrawn` records with a reason, country, year and reference. ChEMBL `molecule` does carry `withdrawn_flag` only, as recorded — the fields live on a different endpoint, which was not checked.
- `scripts/revamp/withdrawn_map.py` → `data/sources/withdrawn/{mapped.parquet,coverage.json,withdrawal-gap.csv,name-candidates-for-review.csv}`: 1,062 rows, field `withdrawal`, over **307 pages (T1 301 / T2 4 / T3 2)**; by rule unii 844 / inchikey 36 / name-candidate 182; 0 records unresolved. Completeness: reason 1,062, country 1,062, year 1,058, source reference 1,056. Every row names ChEMBL as `source_url` and `licence`; no row is attributed to WITHDRAWN. UNII for a ChEMBL molecule is derived from the independent FDA UNII files by InChIKey then exact name, never from the page being matched.
- **Cross-check:** 301 of 307 mapped pages already carry the corpus `withdrawn` flag (6 are new), and 288 are corroborated by Inxight marketing status with zero active records. Inxight supplies a jurisdiction and record dates but no withdrawal decision date or reason, so it yields neither a year nor a reason on its own. `withdrawal-gap.csv` supersedes the 2026-09-05 version. LICENSES row appended (WITHDRAWN, not ingested); no ChEMBL row added, ChEMBL 37 is already recorded.

Licence CC BY 4.0, quoted verbatim in the manifest; re-run reports 10 files, 10 unchanged, no refetch. LICENSES rows appended for both the register and the TGA site.
- `scripts/revamp/tga_susmp_parse.py` → `data/sources/tga-artg/2026-09-06/parsed/susmp-entries.json`: 4,278 schedule entries across Schedules 2–10 (2: 165, 3: 100, 4: 2,394, 5: 480, 6: 572, 7: 216, 8: 85, 9: 173, 10: 93; Schedule 1 is intentionally blank in the Standard), read from the instrument's own paragraph styles (`ActHead1` headings, `PoisonsStandardScheduleEntry` entries, `Paragraph`/`…EntryParagraph`/`…EntryCaveat` qualifications), with `w:noBreakHyphen` restored so systematic names keep their hyphens. Each entry keeps its verbatim text, its qualifications, its Appendix D `#` mark and any asterisked trivial name. 18 systematic names the parser cannot close (a bond hyphen or a lowercase-bearing bracket, all pesticides and designer drugs) are listed in `coverage.json.parserOpenNameExamples` and matched to nothing.
- `scripts/revamp/tga_susmp_map.py` → `data/sources/tga-artg/{mapped.parquet,coverage.json,name-candidates-for-review.json,unmatched-records.json}`: 3,549 rows over 2,929 pages (T1 787 / T2 1,543 / T3 599), field `regulatory.AU`; by rule unii 3,096 / inchikey 25 / skeleton 32 (linked `form_of`, never merged, 28 pages) / name-candidate 396. 2,697 pages are identifier-confirmed and 232 rest on an unconfirmed name only. The Standard carries no UNII, InChIKey, CAS or structure, so every entry name goes through the FDA UNII name lists first (types of/cn/sys, brand and code excluded) exactly as the EMA and Orange Book steps do. Spot-checked against the instrument text: paracetamol in Schedules 2, 3 and 4; cannabis and psilocybine in 8 and 9; alprazolam and ketamine in 8; metformin, sirolimus, rofecoxib and semaglutide in 4. 161 pages carry a Schedule 8 or 9 entry and 136 an Appendix D mark, which is what operating rule 9's controlled-substance rendering keys on.

- ARTG half BLOCKED-WITH-EVIDENCE (`data/revamp/worklog-entries/2.12-blocker.md`, summarised in `docs/revamp/BLOCKERS.md`): the TGA Copyright page states "You must not use the whole or any part of the content on this website for any commercial purposes" and "You are not permitted to re-transmit, distribute or commercialise the material without our prior written approval"; `www.ebs.tga.gov.au`, which serves the ARTG search and export, returns `User-agent: * / Disallow: /`; and `www.tga.gov.au` timed out on all nine requests over two days (`curl: (28) … 0 bytes received`, http_code 000, TLS handshake completing first, apex and second edge IP checked). data.gov.au holds no ARTG dataset and the Internet Archive holds no bulk export. Every `regulatory.AU` value therefore carries `artgStatus: null` with the reason; nothing renders as "not found in the ARTG", because the register was never searched.

### [2026-09-05T20:52:47+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.13 — EMA centrally authorised medicines (EMA reuse notice)

### [2026-09-05T20:52:47+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.14 — MHRA products database and emc (UK): BLOCKED-WITH-EVIDENCE, not scraped

- Terms checked before any data request and recorded verbatim: `bash scripts/revamp/fetch_log.sh` for robots.txt on products.mhra.gov.uk (no `Disallow`), medicines.org.uk (`/cdn-cgi/` only) and gov.uk, then the emc Legal & Privacy Notice, emc About, MHRA About/Cookies/Accessibility, GOV.UK terms and OGL v3 → `data/sources/mhra-emc/2026-09-06/legal/` (35 files, 6,061,391 bytes, SHA256 each in `manifest.json`; re-run verifies 35 unchanged, 0 refetched). emc forbids commercial use, populating a database from the site, re-circulation and systematic update tracking; products.mhra.gov.uk publishes no terms at any route (its Next.js build manifest enumerates all 14 routes) and SmPC text is marketing-authorisation-holder copyright, which OGL excludes and MHRA does not license here. Determination with every quotation: `legal/TERMS-DETERMINATION.md`.

- Forgone scope measured, not estimated: `raw/products-mhra-sitemap.xml` (4,973,488 bytes, `lastmod` 2026-05-20) lists 25,414 URLs — 21,589 product pages carrying SmPC 4.5 and 5.1, 3,769 substance pages — and is joined to nothing. LICENSES.md row appended with all eight columns; blocker at `data/revamp/worklog-entries/2.14-blocker.md` and appended to `docs/revamp/BLOCKERS.md`.

### [2026-09-05T20:52:47+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.15 — PMDA (Japan)

Legal first: `www.pmda.go.jp` serves no `robots.txt` (HTTP 404, response and finding in `data/sources/pmda/2026-09-06/legal/robots-check.json`); the authoritative Japanese Site Policy (`legal/site-policy-ja.html`, English machine translation in `legal/site-policy-en.html`) puts all site content under Public Data License 1.0 — attribution and an edited-content statement required, 商用利用も可能です, no share-alike (`legal/public-data-license-1.0.html`) — while its download note prohibits bulk, frequent and automated crawling downloads and requires the minimum necessary. PMDA publishes the whole English approved-products list as one consolidated PDF, so the pull is four requests to pmda.go.jp plus one for the licence text, no crawling; every request is logged in `data/sources/pmda/2026-09-06/requests.log`.

Retrieved (`scripts/revamp/pmda_fetch.py`): 5 files, 3,442,569 bytes, SHA256 and licence per file in `data/sources/pmda/2026-09-06/manifest.json`; the data file is `raw/new-drugs-approved-2004-04-to-2026-02.pdf` (3,206,009 bytes, sha256 e1771f98…). Parsed (`scripts/revamp/pmda_parse.py`): 207 pages, 205 tables, 21 header wordings resolved by role, 276 doubled-glyph tokens and 44 unmapped `(cid:)` glyphs repaired, 77 merged ingredient cells and 35 split-off fragments reattached → 2,525 approval rows, every row dated, 2004–Feb 2026, in `parsed/approvals.ndjson` with counts in `parsed/parse-report.json`.

Mapped (`scripts/revamp/pmda_map.py`; three JAN counter-ions appended to `scripts/revamp/salts.txt`): PMDA carries no UNII or structure, so ingredient names are resolved against `data/corpus-20k/raw/fda-unii/UNII_Names_4Aug2026.txt` first as written and then normalised. 1,348 of 28,832 pages gain `regulatory.JP` — Tier 1 353, Tier 2 901, Tier 3 94 — from 3,558 rows in `data/sources/pmda/mapped.parquet`: 1,199 pages confirmed by UNII, 1 skeleton linked `form_of` and never merged, 157 name-only. A name resolving to several UNIIs on several pages is not treated as confirmed; it goes to review, which is why 307 rows across 157 pages sit in `name-candidates-for-review.json`.

DrugCentral cross-check (`data/sources/pmda/drugcentral-pmda-crosscheck.json`): 629 pages carry `regulatory.JP` from both, 122 only from DrugCentral, 719 only from PMDA; 200 of those PMDA-only pages have their only Japanese approval after DrugCentral's 2023-11-01 release, and both full lists are in the file. Honest limits: 250 of 2,525 rows reach no page (`unmatched-records.json`) — 225 name vaccines, immunoglobulins, allergen extracts and Japan-only new molecules absent from the corpus, 25 are rows where PMDA itself names no active ingredient; the list covers new-drug approvals from April 2004 only, so an older or generic Japanese approval is absent, not negative.

### [2026-09-05T20:52:47+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.16 — PharmGKB/ClinPGx (CC BY-SA 4.0) and CPIC (CC0 1.0)

- `scripts/revamp/pharmgkb_cpic_fetch.py` then `scripts/revamp/pharmgkb_cpic_manifest.py` → `data/sources/pharmgkb-cpic/2026-09-06/{raw,legal,manifest.json,requests.log}`: 11 legal artefacts (five robots.txt, the ClinPGx data usage policy that CPIC's own licence URL redirects to in both saved and rendered form, the licence file shipped inside the archives) then seven ClinPGx bulk ZIPs at the 30 s crawl-delay api.clinpgx.org asks for, then the CPIC PostgREST tables guideline/pair/drug (29 / 635 / 324 rows) — 21 files, 6,426,322 bytes, SHA256 each. PharmGKB is now ClinPGx: api.pharmgkb.org no longer resolves and the download host is api.clinpgx.org. A re-run refetched 0 files and verified every SHA256 against the manifest.
- `scripts/revamp/pharmgkb_cpic_map.py` → `data/sources/pharmgkb-cpic/{mapped.parquet,coverage.json,name-candidates-for-review.json,unmatched-records.json}`: 21,678 rows over 1,309 pages (T1 383 / T2 801 / T3 125); by rule unii 1,147 pages, skeleton 39 (linked `form_of`, never merged), inchikey 9, name-candidate 149; 1,156 pages identifier-confirmed. Two consecutive runs produce a byte-identical parquet and an identical coverage file apart from its timestamp.
- Fields gained: `pgx` on 634 pages (T1 200 / T2 380 / T3 54, 7,054 clinical annotations with gene, variant, level of evidence 1A–4, score, phenotype category and phenotype text), `pgxCpicGuideline` on 332 pages (634 CPIC gene-drug pairs: guideline existence, name, URL, genes and CPIC level — existence and gene only, no dosing and no recommendation table retrieved), `pgxDrugLabel` on 576 pages (1,652 regulator label annotations), `pgxGeneAssociations` on 1,293 pages (12,338 curated gene-chemical relationships). Level of evidence cross-checked between summary_annotations.tsv and clinicalVariants.tsv: 5,191 of 5,191 rows agree on variant, gene set and level.
- Mapping note: PharmGKB publishes no UNII (one cross-reference in 5,313 records), so rule (a) resolves it through `data/corpus-20k/raw/fda-unii/UNII_Records_4Aug2026.txt` by full InChIKey, then PubChem CID, then RxNorm CUI, each required to return exactly one UNII, with the route on every row at `value.match.uniiResolvedBy`. This matters because PharmGKB stores some structures in a different tautomer from FDA and PubChem — warfarin is QTXVAVXCBMYBJW-UHFFFAOYSA-N in PharmGKB against PJVWKTKQMONHTI-UHFFFAOYSA-N in FDA UNII 5Q7ZVV76EI — and the InChI hydrogen layer sits inside the first InChIKey block, so such a record matches neither by full key nor by skeleton and is reached by the CID or RxNorm route instead.
- Honest limits: 2,596 of 5,313 PharmGKB chemicals reach no page (1,319 metabolites, 1,095 drug classes excluded from the name route by design, 121 drug- or prodrug-typed records the corpus does not hold), listed in `unmatched-records.json`; 16 of 324 CPIC drugs reach no page; 362 name candidates go to the Phase 3 review list, 30 of them with an independent PubChem CID or RxNorm agreement recorded, ordered agreement-first then by tier. LICENSES row appended to `docs/data/LICENSES.md`. Two licence facts need Felix: ClinPGx adds "Under no circumstances can ClinPGx data be sold for other's private or commercial use" and a research-purpose term on top of CC BY-SA 4.0, and CC BY-SA 4.0 share-alike attaches to any page rendering this data; CPIC states its acronym may not be reproduced on another website without NIH permission.

### [2026-09-05T20:52:47+00:00] Phase 2 Step 2.18 — DDInter 2.0 (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, validation only, joined to nothing)

- `scripts/revamp/ddinter_fetch.py 2026-09-06`, then `ddinter_fetch_interactions.py 2026-09-06 <k>/3` (three workers, 0.7 s apart), `ddinter_fetch_descriptions.py`, `ddinter_finalise_manifest.py` → `data/validation/ddinter/2026-09-06/{raw,legal,verification,manifest.json,requests.log}`: 38 files / 196,621,610 bytes, SHA256 each; 2,348 logged requests, 2,343 × HTTP 200 and 5 × 404 (the absent robots.txt and two wrong-path probes), zero fetch failures. `legal/terms.html` and the missing `robots.txt` were retrieved before the first data request; the licence is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 and the terms permit download "for your own personal, non-commercial, informational or scholarly use", so the non-commercial gate in `docs/revamp/BLOCKERS.md` (Felix's confirmation, Phase 4.1) governs every use of this pull and nothing here reaches a page.
- The eight ATC CSVs on the Download page hold 160,235 distinct pairs, not the 302,516 records the statistics page states: a pair whose two drugs both sit in an ATC class with no CSV (C, G, J, M, N, S) appears in no file — amlodipine carries 289 partners in the CSVs against 802 at `/server/interact-with/DDInter79/`. The site's own JSON endpoints completed it: 2,290 of 2,290 drugs read, 302,515 distinct interaction ids against the stated 302,516, and every CSV pair present (`pairs_only_in_bulk_csv: 0`).
- `scripts/revamp/ddinter_index.py 2026-09-06` → `data/validation/ddinter/{index.parquet,interaction-descriptions.parquet,name-unii-candidates.parquet,unmatched-drugs.csv,coverage.json}`: 295,184 pairs (Moderate 189,427 / Major 51,003 / Unknown 42,415 / Minor 12,339) keyed by DDInter id, drug name and DrugBank id with the seven mechanism flags; severity agrees with the CSVs on 160,221 of 160,235 shared pairs. Two consecutive runs write byte-identical parquet (index sha256 7ef41093…). No `mapped.parquet` and nothing under `data/sources/`.
- Candidates only, never a join: DDInter publishes no UNII, InChIKey or structure identifier, so rules (a) and (b) cannot be applied from this source and all 6,351 rows (4,565 name-candidate, 1,786 DrugBank-id) are unconfirmed. 2,229 of 2,290 drugs reach a corpus page, 2,222 reach a corpus UNII, touching 2,888 pages (T1 892 / T2 1,780 / T3 216). The 61 unmatched are vaccines, antivenins, immune globulins and radioiodides (`unmatched-drugs.csv`). `scripts/revamp/salts.txt` was read and deliberately not extended: the 285 unmatched names end in route and formulation qualifiers — `(topical)` 90, `(ophthalmic)` 62, `(nasal)` 20 — which are not counter-ions, so `ddinter_index.py` strips a trailing parenthesised qualifier locally and keeps it in its own column, recovering 222 of them.
- Defect found and fixed before publication: a first build joined the pair `interaction_id` to the description table `id` and gave 8,371 pairs a sentence about different drugs (Abacavir–Chlorpromazine carried the flumazenil/tricyclic text). The two are separate id spaces — pairs 1–302,665, descriptions −1–8,576 — evidenced in `data/validation/ddinter/2026-09-06/verification/interaction-id-space.md`. The text join is gone; descriptions ship unjoined and the pair table carries severity and mechanism flags. LICENSES row appended, marked validation only, non-commercial, never joined. `origin` is a public repository, so one `.gitignore` line keeps `data/validation/ddinter/*.parquet` on the workstation — pushing 295,184 CC BY-NC-SA rows from a commercial project would be redistribution — while the manifest, licence capture, request log, verification notes and `coverage.json` stay tracked as Gate G2 evidence.

### [2026-09-06] Phase 3 Step 3.4 — second-opinion adjudication prepared for the lead

Command: `.venv-corpus/bin/python scripts/revamp/identity_adjudicate.py`

Outputs:
- `data/revamp/identity/second-opinion-classified.csv` (680 rows)
- `data/revamp/identity/apply-list.csv` (13 rows over 10 distinct pairs)
- `data/revamp/identity/hold-list.csv` (64 pairs, ordered by reach then tier)
- `data/revamp/identity-review.md` (the lead's draft, one section per held pair)
- `data/revamp/identity/adjudication-summary.json` (counts, reach split, issues)

The script joins `identity/remainder.csv` and `identity/second-opinion.csv` row for row (order and pair
identity asserted), then classifies each second verdict by whether a rule already written in
`docs/specs/identity-resolution.md` licenses applying it on the evidence that second opinion cites.
Every cited identifier is confirmed against the corpus's own record in
`identity/spine-attached.parquet` before it can license anything.

Result over 680 rows (644 distinct pairs): LICENSED-MERGE 6, LICENSED-FORM_OF 7,
LICENSED-SEPARATE 453, NOT-LICENSED 214. The 214 divide into 150 rows that record a merge identity
pass 2/3 already put in force — confirmed for all 150 against `presence-applicable.ndjson`, the
merged-away key absent and the survivor live — and **64 pairs a person still has to decide**: 23
touching an indexable page, 7 the residual band, 34 the rest. At 60 seconds a pair that is **64
minutes, under two hours**, with no further rule-derived reduction needed.

Nothing was applied. Recommended defaults in the draft come only from the spec: a merge the rules do
not license defaults to separate (§1), a form_of defaults to the existing state.

Four points for the lead:
1. Eight merge rows at high confidence rest on the sentence "one page holds no structure of its own"
   while the spine records a full InChIKey on both pages with different skeletons. All eight are held.
2. §2 reserves a K1/K2 disagreement for a person, so all 29 non-separate rows whose first-pass reason
   is that hold stay held whatever the second verdict — including 15 `form_of` verdicts that cite a
   GSRS ester relation (clobetasol propionate, cortisone acetate, megestrol acetate and so on).
3. `HOLD:existing:anhydrous-citric-acid` and `HOLD:existing:potassium-citrate` sit on both lists;
   apply the licensed merge before their held pair is reopened.
4. Eight apply-list rows come from pairs the first pass held as a whole chain because an exception
   class separates another link; applying them needs the transitive closure checked first.

The residual band had no recorded definition in `data/revamp`, so the script derives one from
`thresholds.json` and states it in the draft: a tier with no derived threshold contributes the pages
at or above its own `ruleAnswerAfterStubFloor` (tier 2 at 8 present fields, measured positional
0.138846 against the 0.20 line and lexical 0.412005 against the 0.353 line). Tier 1 holds a threshold
of 7, so its rule-answer set is the indexable set; tier 3 has no rule answer.

**Measurement (§4).** First batch, 30 hubs (7 pathway — every pathway hub the corpus holds — and 23
target), every pair scored exactly: positional median **0.0793** against the 0.20 line, lexical
median **0.3354** against 0.353. Every hub, 972 pages in three seeded size-matched folds of 324
(`scripts/corpus-20k/gate2/folds.py`, the Gate 2 construction): positional **0.1250**, lexical
**0.3266**. Both sets clear both lines.

**The first batch failed the lexical line first, at 0.3566, and the templates were fixed, not the
line.** The decomposition is in the measurement: for the median pair (`target/andr`↔`target/nr3c1`)
the synthesis word sets overlapped at 0.459 while the member-name lists overlapped at 0.222 — the
repetition was template scaffolding, not shared membership. Four changes, all of them adding
recorded values or removing words that carry none: H5 names the three oldest completed-without-
posted-results registrations instead of one (an NCT id is a near-unique token); H2 names five
stopped members instead of three and drops "reason unrecorded"/"place unrecorded", which are
absences and so furniture under `docs/specs/phase4-generators.md` §11; H4 drops "mechanism … inside
the hub"; H6 names the three most potent members instead of one. Median lexical fell to 0.3354.

**Three defects in the hub set were found and fixed on the way.** (a) Target grouping keyed on the
raw UniProt accession split one protein across species — P35348 (human alpha-1A) and P43140 (rat)
became two hubs with 65% shared compounds — so grouping now keys on the UniProt entry-name prefix,
which is shared across orthologues, and falls back to gene symbol then the raw key
(`target_group_key`). (b) The overlap merge was single-linkage and chained: a ~0.5 edge from the
carbonic anhydrases to ABCB1 and another from ABCB1 to polyphenol oxidase 2 published one page
naming eleven unrelated proteins as one target. It is now complete-linkage — a group joins a
component only when it meets 0.5 Jaccard against every group already in it — and the hub's printed
name and the first protein in its definition are now the same protein (the largest group leads).
(c) Member slugs were derived from display names and 4,567 of 6,124 named no published page
(`/d/k1-03j11k103c` for algeldrate); the build now reads the route map
`data/revamp/identity/page-slugs.csv`, so the hub links and prints exactly what the member page
publishes.

A fifth template change followed from reading the output rather than the measurement: a recorded
name can contain a comma of its own ("PROPAFENONE, (R)-", and the combination page
"Sitagliptin, Metformin"), so a comma-separated list of names was ambiguous. Every name list in the
synthesis and in the measured text now separates with a middle dot. The medians did not move: a
separator is not a word token.

### [2026-09-08T05:44:49+00:00] Phase 4 — fix round 2 (§12) and the Phase 5 hub integration

**§12(1) The classification-absence answer is furniture wherever it renders.** `BlockBody` now
carries a `furniture` flag parallel to its paragraphs; `buildBlockBody` marks
"No regulator classification is recorded for X" in both branches that write it (the `classification`
block and the supervision block's fallback); `renderPage` pushes the paragraph with that flag, so it
leaves the furniture-free text `page_text_v5` writes by default; `CorpusParagraph` carries it to
`QuestionBlock.tsx`, which paints `data-furniture="true"`, so `rendered_dup_check.py` skips it with
the register absence table and `slop_draw.py` excludes it from test (b). New case in
`tests/test_render_safety.py`: every recorded instance is marked, the page still paints it, and no
line of the furniture-free render carries it.

**§12(2) Test (b) is evaluated over the masked block.** `ProvenanceEntry` gains `group`, named by
the render itself at each region boundary (`question:q3`, `computed`, `registration`, …), so the
draw groups the sentences a reader meets under one question and no others rather than inferring the
unit from the sentence's own shape. `slop_draw.py` builds a corpus-wide **block** census from the
provenance map (in-scope groups only: the question blocks and the Tier 3 computed section), masks
each block's sentences in painted order and joins them, and fails a block carried by more than 0.5 %
of pages. A one-sentence block is its own sentence and is held to the literal rule by construction.
The sentence-level literal reading is computed beside it and reported as `failBSentenceLiteral`,
never as the gate. The render/DOM comparison is no longer written twice: `slop_draw.py` imports
`dom_parity.py`'s extraction, fold and comparison, `dom_parity.py` hides `[data-furniture]` with the
rest of the markup and reads the furniture-free render, so one number describes parity.

**§12(3) Same-name pairs, partitioned.** `identity_apply.py` finds 189 families of live pages that
print one name and serve `<slug>` and `<slug>-<n>`, ranks them K1 > K2 > K3 > K4 with the unsuffixed
slug breaking a tie, and moves every registry study whose every recorded intervention on a losing
page is that shared name to the ranked page: **200 pages partitioned, 5,281 studies moved off 102 of
them**; 2,799 studies stayed because their interventions named more than the shared name and 78
pages record no study at all.

**§12(4) Combination products sharing a component.** A combination page is a `COMBO:{…}` key or a
proper name carrying the FDA's own conjunction; 119 such pages, **71 of them share a component with
another, across 149 pairs**. A study stays where its interventions name the product's full component
set, moves to the sibling whose set they name, or goes to the component page that records the same
study: **787 studies moved off 8 pages**, including the 41 and 25 that `ABP 980` and `CT-P6` — codes
that sit on the shared trastuzumab UNII — had put on Herceptin Hylecta and Phesgo. The Phesgo study
whose intervention reads "Phesgo (trastuzumab og pertuzumab)" stays on Phesgo. Each of the 71 pages
now opens with `Recorded components: A · B · C.`

**§12(5) 351(a) merges.** The FDA Purple Book licenses **109** suffixed proper names under `351(a)`
whose UNII is the unsuffixed INN page's; R1 licenses the merge and the biosimilar exception does not
apply. Those pages merged into the INN page (proper name kept as a synonym, slug redirected), taking
the corpus from 28,818 to **28,709** live pages. `biosimilar_of` is now kept only where a `351(k)`
row licenses the page: **81 edges kept**, 76 removed by the merges and 1 by the licence test.
`redirect-plan-v5.csv` holds **174 rows**; `redirect_check.py --plan` over it: universe 9,857, 8,863
served directly, 1,044 by one hop, **0 orphans, 0 chains, 0 conflicts — PASS**.

**§12(6) Hub integration.** The member page's Hubs row (`CorpusHubRow` on the view model,
`components/dossier/corpus/HubRows.tsx`, rendered after `RelationsRows` on the full page and inside
`StubRecord`, which is the noindex leaf the row exists for); the `hubs.xml` sitemap child
(`SITEMAP_CHILDREN`, `hubsSitemapEntries`, the `sitemapChildEntries` branch and the
`populatedSitemapChildren` guard, with `countHubs()` added to `lib/hubs/queries.ts`);
`scripts/revamp/hubs_load.ts` sequenced after `materialise.ts` and documented as a Phase 7 section
of `docs/specs/deployment-plan.md`; `/h` linked from the site footer as "Compare by target, class or
pathway", the home page's frozen bar untouched; and §10's display-name rule applied in
`materialise.ts`, `page_text_v5.ts` and `hubs_build.py` from one shared implementation, so the
dossier, the measured text and the hub print one name.

**§10 display names.** `printedDisplayName` replaces an all-caps register string with a `common`,
`inn` or `merged-page` synonym that names the same record — the register's bracketed vocabulary tag
removed, and a `merged-page` name accepted only where it is the display name with the register's
trailing qualifier removed. `CLOBETASOL` → `Clobetasol`, `POTASSIUM CITRATE ANHYDROUS` →
`Potassium Citrate`. A synonym naming a shorter substance (`Dapagliflozin` on
`DAPAGLIFLOZIN PROPANEDIOL`) is a different record and never replaces the register string.

**Four defects the parity check found on the way, all fixed in the generator.**

1. **`hubs_load.ts` could not find its own inputs.** `new URL(import.meta.url).pathname`
   percent-encodes a space, so under a checkout named "Claude Projects" the loader resolved
   `.../Claude%20Projects/...` and failed with `ENOENT` on `hubs.ndjson`; the hub tables stayed
   empty and `sitemap.xml` correctly advertised no `hubs.xml`. `fileURLToPath` decodes it.
2. **A question block with an empty body painted its heading.** §1 forbids a heading over an empty
   body and `renderPage` drops such a block; `lib/corpus/dossier-page.ts` did not, so
   `/d/sodium-carbonate` painted "More Sodium Carbonate was worse in human: at what point?" with
   nothing under it. The page now drops a block with no paragraph and no row, exactly as the render
   does.
3. **Two controlled-substance schedules rendered in two orders.** `page_controlled` has no order of
   its own; the template sorts by jurisdiction, list and schedule, and the render used the stored
   order. The render now applies the same sort (§11: the render mirrors the painted order).
4. **The render named counterparts the merges had absorbed.** `page_text_v5` took its page universe
   and its name map from the tier map, which still names every page the corpus-20k run created; the
   loader takes both from the identity revision. After the 109 §12 merges the render was naming
   `Insulin Lispro-Aabc` as an interaction counterpart on pages whose own record had merged into
   `Insulin lispro`. The render now covers exactly the keys `--identity` holds, and names a
   counterpart only where that revision holds a page for it.

**What each item changed.**

1. **Absences never appear in a prose answer.** The `classification` question is retired: it fired
   only on an S10-only record and answered "No regulator classification is recorded for X", on
   16,814 pages. `jurisdiction` (seed 17) now fires only where a register recorded an affirmative
   classification — 12,872 → **397** pages — and names only those statuses. The supervision answer
   names a schedule, a withdrawal, a boxed warning or a REMS and never a jurisdiction read from
   another source; where the record holds none, the block does not render. The label question's
   regulatory paragraph is gone, and with it the `curatedMarketingStatusNote` leak:
   `readRegisterStatuses` accepts only a two-letter jurisdiction code, so a stored note is no longer
   read as a jurisdiction. `registerStatusValues` and `registerStatusRows` drop absences; the
   registration block's absence table is the one place they are stated. Questions 98,846 → **69,530**.
2. **"What the registers record" is retired.** `withdrawnArcRows` and `WithdrawnArc.tsx` are gone.
   `registerEventLines` folds the events into the registration block, one sentence per event in
   words — `Withdrawn in France, 1996, for "drug misuse" (ChEMBL; Open Targets)` — with identical
   events from several registers merged onto one line and the registers' own column names
   (`drug_warning`, `withdrawn_flag`, `warningType`) never printed. The register's stated reason is
   quoted, because it is the register speaking: several notices read "Not safe or effective for
   intended use", which unquoted would be this site calling something unsafe.
3. **Interactions render once, grouped.** `page_blocks.py` groups the curated enzyme and transporter
   rows by role before either consumer reads them: **15,382 rows → 4,497 lines**, one per role, with
   the shared family prefix written once — `Curated · Substrate of CYP1A2, 2A6, 2B6, 2C19, 2C8, 2C9,
   2D6, 2E1 and 3A4, with a reported magnitude · Inxight FRDB`. The per-line badge and the per-tier
   heading are gone, so the tier label is one visible element per line. Record ids are in the closed
   disclosure only, and the block never paints the same line twice.
4. **Schedules render once.** The Singapore line names the class and says "see the
   controlled-substance schedules below"; the Australian line names the schedule and does the same.
   Neither repeats the statute, its version or the substance as listed — those are in the table.
5. **Trial lists cap at six visible rows.** The counted remainder is a closed native `<details>`
   whose summary is the group's own label, so its rows are not painted until a reader opens them.
6. **The Inxight "unspecified" rows and the upstream-register clauses are disclosure only.**
   `build_blocks.py` marks the row `disclosed` (15,140 of 15,966 "Other registers" rows) and moves
   "upstream registers: ClinicalTrials, February 2021 …" into the row's disclosure. Migration 0031
   adds `page_registration.disclosed`.
7. **Single-value statements are rows.** `BlockBody` gains `facts`, painted under the question
   heading and above the prose: "Posted no result · 25 of 29 completed trials", "Publications
   (ChEMBL) · 1981–2023". "X has no recorded human exposure" is furniture.
8. **The nearest approved neighbour is rows** — `Closest approved compound · Flurazepam · similarity
   0.48 · approved US, CA · generic available` — with a prose sentence only where the MCS comparison
   named the substituent that differs.
9. **A form-of note prints the related name once**, as the link text inside its own sentence.
10. **The header prints one evidence line**: the evidence kind where the record holds human data,
    "No human study recorded" where it does not, never both.
11. **The decorative glyphs are CSS pseudo-element content** (`.cd-glyph::before`,
    `.cd-anchor-glyph::before`), so neither the ruler nor a crawler reads "◇" or "~" as a word.
12. **SUSMP matching requires a full normalised synonym.** `normalise_name` strips trailing
    counter-ion words, which collapses "SODIUM PHOSPHATE", "SODIUM DIACETATE" and "SODIUM CITRATE"
    all to "sodium"; that is how the trisodium citrate page came to carry Schedules 3, 4 and 5.
    `corpus_join` gains `full_name_to_keys` and the mapping rule (d) uses it: **193 rows removed, 0
    added** (3,549 → 3,356; name-candidate 396 → 203). `integrate_sources.py` was re-run at
    `--run-date 2026-09-06` so nothing else moved, and the controlled block was rebuilt.
13. **Hubs deduplicate by member set.** Complete linkage at 0.5 Jaccard over the finished hubs of
    every kind; the largest member set survives, the others become aliases that redirect to it and
    are named in its definition line ("Also known by …"). **972 built → 923 published, 49 aliases.**
    `hub_aliases` (migration 0031) carries them and `/h/<type>/<slug>` answers with a permanent
    redirect. An absorbed hub's members that the survivor did not already hold join it, with the
    membership evidence recording which absorbed group put them there: `docs/specs/hubs.md` §3
    forbids an indexable leaf with no hub, and four pages whose only group was absorbed had none
    until this was added (`link_graph_check.py --data-only` PASS, 13,121 memberships over 6,124
    pages). Re-measured: `measure-all-v2.json` positional **0.125**, lexical **0.3208**, both under
    the lines; `measure-first-batch-v2.json` passes.
14. **Duplicate hold.** `scripts/revamp/duplicate_holds.py` reads the rendered check's own outputs
    and holds the page with fewer own facts: `trastuzumab-and-hyaluronidase-oysk` (20 own fields) is
    `noindex,follow` and links `pertuzumab-trastuzumab-and-hyaluronidase-zzxf` (21), which is the
    pair §12(4) predicted would survive. `data/revamp/identity/duplicate-holds.csv`, appended to
    `hold-list-v5.csv` with the default "one page per product". Migration 0031 adds
    `corpus_pages.duplicate_hold_of`; the loader reads the file and refuses the held page an index.

**The render.** `data/revamp/render-v8/{text,text-with-furniture,provenance,provenance-with-furniture}`,
**28,657 pages**; furniture-free **10,274,502 words**, mean 358.5; with furniture 14,265,280 and
497.8. `render-v7` deleted.

**Tests.** `tests/test_render_safety.py` gains eight §13 rules — no absence in a prose answer, no
field name or enum string in visible text, one tier label per line, no dataset record id on a prose
line, a statute row once, a trial list of six, no decorative glyph, and a Poisons Standard row that
matches a full synonym — and the retired classification answer is now asserted absent while the
registration block's absence table is asserted present. `tests/test_hub_dedupe.py` is new (6 cases
on the member-set rule, complete linkage included). `tests/unit/corpus-render-safety.test.ts` gains
the same rules on the components (52 cases).

**Two defects the new rules found and fixed on the way.** Seed 8 records the ChEMBL first-approval
event's `date` as the stringified source record, which printed as "first approval {'ch" once
`slice(0, 4)` had taken four characters of it; the renderer now takes a date only where the value is
shaped like one and falls back to the event's own year. And the same block appended the current
register state to its answer, which on a never-cleared register read "; not cleared".

### Phase 6 — step 6.1, the RSC payload

**What the measurement said, and why the primary option was not available.** The audit in
`data/revamp/payload-audit-before.json` (108 pages: the eight samples of step 0.3 and a 100-page
seeded draw from the build's own indexable set) found the dossier shipped twice — the flight stream
a median **54.5 %** of the served document, repeating **100 %** of the rendered record's text, live
text-to-HTML **0.048** against a 0.15 floor. Step 6.1's first instruction is to render the dossier
server-side and pass the client only the search bar's state. **That was already true**: every
component under `components/dossier/corpus/` was a server component, and the one client component,
the rail marker, took no props. The duplicate is not a component-boundary fault. Next.js inlines the
page's rendered element tree into the document on every render path a page can take —
`createInlinedDataReadableStream` is called unconditionally in
`next/dist/server/app-render/app-render.js` — and no configuration removes it. So the fallback the
step names was taken, in the form the corpus can carry: the two route families leave the page
pipeline and the server writes their documents itself, rather than 28,657 files being exported to
disk.

**The `'use client'` audit, since the step asks for it.** Thirty-one modules carried the directive;
thirty do now, and exactly one of them was in a corpus record's tree: `components/dossier/corpus/ContentsRailMarker.tsx`, which
took no props and read the DOM. It is deleted; its behaviour is `lib/island/rail.ts`. Every other
component under `components/dossier/corpus/` and `components/hubs/` was already a server component,
and their only client dependency was `next/link`, which is itself a client component and cannot be
rendered outside the page pipeline — eight files now use a plain `<a>`, which is also what a
document with no client router should emit. The rest of the directive's users are the React shell
(`AppShell`, `app-context`, `SiteHeader`, the three modals, `GoogleAnalytics`) and the pages that
need it — the review queue, the legacy dossier's contribution and comment controls, the home search.
A corpus document uses none of them: its header and footer are server components
(`components/document/`), and the search field, the rail marker and the analytics choice are the one
island. So the flight duplicate was never a component-boundary fault, and no amount of converting
would have removed it.

**A second measurement decided how much markup had to go with it, and separates this step's effect
from fix round 3's.** `payload-audit-before.json` was written against the v7 build, which predates
fix round 3. The build this step started from was re-measured over the same 108 pages in a browser
before a line was changed: live text-to-HTML **0.0467**, flight stream a median 64.6 % of the live
document, `<main>` 78,659 bytes, head 3,757, other scripts 3,023, header and footer 3,691,
`innerText` 11,534. Removing the flight stream and changing nothing else would have given
**0.1318** — under the floor. So the document's own shell and the record's markup had to come down
too, and the delta below is this step's plus fix round 3's, with the flight stream accounting for
0.0467 → 0.1318 of it and the markup for the rest.

**What was built.**

- `app/d/[slug]/route.ts`, `app/h/route.ts`, `app/h/[type]/[slug]/route.ts` — route handlers that
  render the record once and return a whole HTML document (`lib/corpus/document.tsx`,
  `lib/hubs/document.tsx`, `components/document/DocumentShell.tsx`, `lib/document/render.tsx`).
  No flight stream, no client chunks, no second copy of any text.
- `lib/island/` → `public/island/rnawiki-document.js`, bundled by `scripts/build-island.mjs` from
  `prebuild` and `predev`: the header's search field, the contents rail's marker, and the analytics
  choice. It is handed the search field's state and nothing of the record. It imports the search
  route, the medicine-type label and the analytics policy from the same modules the React shell
  imports, so the two renderers cannot drift on any of the three.
- `app/legacy-record/[slug]/page.tsx` — the React dossier, for the 29 slugs the corpus does not
  hold and for every end-to-end fixture. `/d/<slug>` forwards to it over loopback and returns
  exactly what it answered (`lib/document/legacy-forward.ts`); the reader's URL, canonical,
  indexing decision, 308 and 404 are unchanged. It answers 404 to anything that is not that
  forward.
- `app/d/[slug]/opengraph-image/route.tsx` — the same card at the same URL, as an explicit route
  now that the segment has no page.
- Markup: the disclosure row lost its `cd-row-head` wrapper and its `cd-row` class (the row is
  `.cd-rows > li`), rail links lost `cd-rail-link` and `data-corpus-rail` (the CSS reaches them
  through the rail; the island reads each link's own `href`), and `data-anchored` is written only
  when it is true. No text changed, no measured class was dropped, and
  `scripts/corpus-20k/gate2/browser-checks.ts` was moved onto the structural selector so its
  rail-link count keeps measuring what it was written to measure (it read 0 for one run; restored,
  it reads 68 focus targets and 102 keyboard stops on metformin).
- `app/layout.tsx` now imports the corpus stylesheets and `lib/fonts.ts`, so the layout's entry in
  the build manifest carries every stylesheet the document links and there is one copy of each face
  in the build.

**Result, same script, same 108 pages** (`payload_audit.py --pages` pins the set, since the
indexable draw would otherwise move with the build):

| | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| live text-to-HTML, all 108 | 0.0481 | **0.15517** |
| live text-to-HTML, 100-page indexable draw | 0.0481 | **0.15399** |
| live text-to-HTML, eight samples | 0.0553 | **0.17639** |
| RSC share of the document | 0.5448 | **0.0** |
| dossier text repeated in the RSC stream | 1.0 | **0.0** |
| median document bytes | 263,156 | **76,312** |

**The floor is met; the 0.25 target is not, and the reason is structural.** A median page's `<main>`
is 66 KB, of which **54 KB is inside closed `<details>`** — the technical disclosures §10 and §13(5)
require to be in the HTML for a reader who opens them and for a crawler that reads them. `innerText`
does not count that text, so it is denominator without numerator. Reaching 0.25 would need the
document at about 46 KB, which is less than the disclosures alone. Deferring them to a fetch would
clear the number and would take the evidence off the page for a reader without scripts; it was not
done. This is a decision for the lead, stated with the numbers rather than resolved here.

**Invariants, re-verified on this build.** `git diff origin/main -- app/robots.ts` empty. Sitemap
index: `tier-1`, `tier-2`, `browse`, `hubs`, `pages`; `sitemaps/tier-3.xml` 404. Redirects
(`redirect-check-v8-local.json`): universe 9,857, 8,863 direct, 1,044 one hop, **0 orphans, 0
chains, 0 conflicts**; live 8,884 × 200 and 973 × 308, **0 failures** — identical to v7. Link graph
(`hubs/link-graph-v8.json`): **PASS**, 919 published hubs all 200, 6,052 member links all 200.
Frozen home search bar (`browser-checks-v8.json`): **0.00 px** against
`data/corpus-20k/gate2/browser-checks-v2.json` on every box value at 1440, 375 and 320 px, same DOM
path. Per-sample accessibility: one `<h1>`, heading order true, no horizontal overflow at 320 px,
contrast unchanged, every summary, rail link and provenance anchor keyboard reachable with a
visible ring.

**The island was exercised in a browser** on the finished build: typing in the header field returns
**10** options in a `role="listbox"` with `aria-expanded="true"`, ArrowDown sets
`aria-activedescendant` to the first option, the first option's `href` is the record it names,
Escape closes the list, scrolling marks **3** rail links `aria-current` (the block and the two group
links beneath it, which is what the class-based marker did), the document contains no
`self.__next_f`, and it has one `<main>` and one JSON-LD block.

**How the local build was made** (the measurement server, not the deployment):
`npm run db:migrate` on a fresh database, `pg_restore --data-only -L <drugs,
inventory_resolutions>` from `rnawiki-backups/corpus-20k-2026-09-05/rnawiki-pre-corpus-20k.pgcustom`
(9,859 rows each), `materialise.ts --tier {1,3,2} --revamp --thresholds
data/revamp/thresholds-v7.json --presence data/revamp/presence-applicable-v8.ndjson`, then
`hubs_load.ts`; 28,657 pages, 298 indexable, 919 hubs. The presence file had to be regenerated —
`derive_threshold.py --applicability-only --fields-dir data/revamp/fields-v2 --out
data/revamp/thresholds-v8.json` → `presence-applicable-v8.ndjson` — because fix round 3 re-ran
`integrate_sources.py` and removed 193 SUSMP rows from `fields-v2`, so `presence-applicable-v7`
now exceeds the loader's present-field count on some pages and trips the
`corpus_pages_present_applicable_count` check. **The v8 ruler's threshold half has not been
re-derived**; the load used v7's per-tier threshold with the current presence numerator, which is
Phase 1's and Phase 4's step, not this one's.

**`npm run gate`: green** after `rm -rf .next/cache` — typecheck, lint, the eight content and
agent checks, `check:seo` (24 files, 279 tests), `format`, `drizzle-kit check`, `test:unit`
(**179 files**), `test:integration` on a disposable database (**26 files, 167 tests**),
`next build`, and Playwright `test:e2e` (**30 passed**), which exercises the loopback forward on
every dossier fixture because those fixtures install a medicine with no corpus row.

**Outputs.** `data/revamp/payload-audit-after.json`, `data/revamp/browser-checks-v8.json`,
`data/revamp/redirect-check-v8-local.json`, `data/revamp/hubs/link-graph-v8.json`.
`docs/specs/deployment-plan.md` gains "The corpus document", which states the build step and the
four things the deployment must now be true of.

Server stopped, disposable database dropped, `.next/cache` and `data/revamp/rendered-text` deleted.

**The loaded build.** 28,657 `corpus_pages`, **298 indexable** (297 Tier 1 and 1 Tier 2 — the ruler
names 298 Tier 1 pages and §13(14)'s duplicate hold takes one of them out of the index), **919 hubs**
published from the 923 the data files hold, and 13,036 hub members. `hubs_load.ts` skipped 4 hubs
and 72 memberships, which is the loader refusing a hub left under five loadable members.

**The ruler, furniture-free (§11's gate figure), against the four earlier rulers.**

| ruler | Tier 1 threshold | indexable | positional (all pairs) | lexical (size-matched) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `thresholds-after` (pre-Phase-4 text) | 12 | 1,061 | 0.160173 | 0.328660 |
| `thresholds-v5` (Phase-4 text, furniture in it) | 23 | 48 | 0.198739 | 0.345081 |

Tier 2 stands at 20-with-one-page on all five rulers. Tier 3 selects none on all five: candidate 3
(19,797 pages) scores 0.600 positional and 0.655 lexical and the smallest candidate that still

retired the classification answer, the register-events block and the repeated interaction and
schedule lines took shared text off every page in the set.

**The with-furniture figures, reported beside the gate and never selecting.** The 298-page set the

Run separately over the with-furniture text as its own ruler
(`thresholds-v8-with-furniture.json`), Tier 1 selects **21** with **230** indexable, positional

**Phase 6.1, confirmed on this build.** `payload_audit.py` over the pinned 108 pages
(`data/revamp/payload-audit-pages.txt`, the same 100 indexable slugs plus the eight step-0.3
samples the step measured before): 108 of 108 measured, RSC dossier share of the document **0.0**,

**0.153994** over the 100-page indexable draw. Both reproduce the step's recorded figures to six

**Slop draws 4 and 5 are not clean, and they fail in two named places.**

| | draw 4 (seed 20260909) | draw 5 (seed 20260910) |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| pages drawn / rendered | 60 / 60 | 60 / 60 |
| sentences · row lines | 1,329 · 3,416 | 1,231 · 3,057 |
| **(a) traces to a stored field** | **16** | **13** |
| **(b) block template on ≤ 0.5 % of pages** | **3** | **2** |
| (b) sentence-level literal, reported beside | 92 | 83 |
| **(c) names a narrative device** | **0** | **0** |
| render/DOM parity, pages in agreement | 30 / 60 | 24 / 60 |

Every (a) failure is one generator and one shape: the grouped curated interaction line §13(3)
introduced — "Curated · Inhibitor of CYP1A1, CYP1A2, … with a reported magnitude · Inxight FRDB" —
carries the whole group's record ids as its trace (`frdb:ddi:16659; frdb:ddi:16660; …`), and the
trace classifier has no class for a list of them, so the line the reader meets cannot be shown to
trace to a stored value. The grouping is right and the trace is real; the provenance map has to
name the class.

The (b) failures are three blocks, two of which appear in both draws: the nearest-neighbour
sentence "it differs from X by a methyl substituent" (168 pages, 0.59 %), the trial-size block
"N at the median, M at the largest across K registered trials" (411 pages, 1.43 %) and, in draw 4
only, a mechanism question block (220 pages, 0.77 %). All three are prose sentences carrying one
computed value, which §13(7) turns into rows everywhere else; these three were missed.

**The render and the DOM no longer agree, and step 6.1's markup trim is why.** Draw 3 measured
0 divergent lines. Draws 4 and 5 measure 367 and 320 lines each way, over 30 and 36 of their 60 pages; a
separate `dom_parity.py` run over the same build, 30 pages at seed 20260909, disagrees on 15. The cause is exact: 6.1 removed the disclosure
row's `cd-row-head` block wrapper, so `<span class="cd-row-label">Ketoconazole</span>` and
`<span class="cd-row-id">A-label-statement · …</span>` are now adjacent inline siblings with no
whitespace between them. CSS separates them for a reader (`.cd-row-label + .cd-row-id` carries
`margin-left: 0.5rem`), so nothing looks wrong on screen, but every text extraction — this check's,
and a crawler's — reads `KetoconazoleA-label-statement`. The render writes the space; the page does
not carry one. This is not one of §4.7's three checks and it does not change the thresholds above,
which are measured on the render, but it does mean the render and the served text differ on those
rows and a crawler reads two fields as one word.

**Two defects in the checks themselves, both found and fixed here, both of the same kind.** Each
named a revision by hand and so read a superseded file the moment the run it was written for ended:
`rendered_dup_check.py` resolved page tiers from `data/revamp/render-v7/pages-all.ndjson` and fell
back to the corpus-20k file, silently, once fix round 3 deleted that render; `link_graph_check.py`
resolved its ruler from the list `("v7", "v6", "v5")` and so measured rule 2 against v7's 301
indexable leaves on a v8 build. Both now take the highest revision present on disk, and rule 2 was
re-run: 299 indexable leaves on the v8 ruler, all seven rules pass.

**One page in the indexable set has no key in the identity revision.** The dup check reports
`acarbose-structure-xufxoaauwzooit` — the K2 InChIKey parent of acarbose, which the loader slugs
from its structure and the check's recorded slug facts still call `acarbose-2`. The page was
rendered and scored like every other (298 requested, 298 rendered, 298 scored), and it flagged no
pair; what it lacks is a key, so a pair involving it could not have been given a reason class.

**What this measurement puts to the lead.**

1. The slop gate's two-consecutive-clean-draws bar is unmet on both draws, and the two causes are
   named above: the grouped curated interaction line's trace has no recognised class (16 and 13),
   and three prose blocks carrying one computed value each sit over the 0.5 % line (3 and 2).
2. Render and DOM disagree on 30 of 60 and 36 of 60 drawn pages because 6.1's markup trim left
   `.cd-row-label` and `.cd-row-id` as adjacent inline siblings. A reader sees the half-rem the CSS
   adds; a text extractor reads the two fields as one word.
3. 1,472 hub-to-hub pairs still measure at or above 0.5 rendered Jaccard on indexable, sitemapped
   pages after §13(13) removed 393 of the 1,865 measured at v7.
4. The comparison set's indexable pairs rose from 25 to 29 while the published set's fell to 0.
   The two sets are different: the published set is 298 pages the v8 ruler names, the comparison
   set is the 1,048 slugged pages of the 1,062 the pre-Phase-4 ruler named, most of which this run
   does not publish.

**What each item changed.**

1. **The supervision answer names the suppression evidence, never a register status.** The block
   read the `regulatory` field jurisdiction by jurisdiction and offered whatever it found as "the
   registers' classification of X"; on Piroxicam that produced "AU scheduled in the Poisons
   Standard", which is a prescription class and not a supervision reason, and whose provenance
   named registers the sentence did not. The answer is now built only from the recorded S1–S9
   classes, in the words `docs/specs/suppression-classes.md` fixes, anchored on the field the class
   evidence came from. `controlled_suppression.py` records **S2 only on the narrow test** the spec
   names — the Singapore Misuse of Drugs Act schedules, a US DEA schedule, an Australian Poisons
   Standard Schedule 8 or 9 — so a Singapore Poisons Act schedule no longer becomes a supervision
   reason: **1,836 pages keep the controlled trigger and the no-dose path, 241 of them carry S2**,
   and the schedule renders in the registration block, which is where §13(4) puts it.
2. **Register application rows leave every question block.** `registerStatusRows` is deleted, and
   `registerStatusValues` drops the record id from its prose ("US approved (2026-09-04)"). The
   supervision, label and register questions paint no application row.
3. **The US status word derives from the application set.** `us_status_word` in `build_blocks.py`:
   any active prescription or over-the-counter application → Approved; a tentative approval with no
   active application → Tentative approval; every application discontinued → Discontinued; a
   recorded withdrawal keeps its own word. **3,082 Approved, 626 Discontinued, 20 Tentative
   approval, 0 contradictions** against 0 lines reading "Approved · N applications: all
   discontinued". The loader counts the contradiction rather than rewriting it, so a load from a
   stale block file says so in its own output.
4. **"Registered studies posting no result" moves to the trials question.** It is a row under
   `trial-history` and `human-data` and is written by the label question no longer.
5. **Label interactions group by label and direction.** `page_blocks.py` groups Tier A by (set id,
   effective time, direction class) and names every counterpart the group stands for:
   **27,120 rows → 5,486 lines**. Piroxicam's twenty-one label lines are three. A counterpart must
   resolve to a substance page or a recognised drug class: `counterpart_artefacts.py` decides the
   two artefact shapes from the registers' own records — a GSRS substance class of
   `structurallyDiverse` (a cell, a tissue, a material, and how `PLATELETS` was linked) and a
   printed name that is a bare abbreviation (`ASA`, the aspirin DL-lysine record the same label
   also names as aspirin) — **3,247 pages named, 1,062 counterpart lines dropped** (725 material,
   337 abbreviation) and recorded in `data/revamp/interactions/counterpart-artefacts.csv`.
6. **A record id is painted only inside a closed disclosure.** `buildBlockBody` strips an identifier
   from every fact, the source list's id carries `cd-source-id` rather than `cd-row-id`, and the
   interaction disclosure row is built by two shared functions (`interactionDisclosureLabel`,
   `interactionRecordIds`) that the corpus renderer and the template both call — the render wrote
   "Substrate of CYP1A1, 1A2 and 2E1 frdb:ddi:12474 · …" while the page painted
   "CuratedB-inxight-frdb", because each had its own idea of what that row said.
7. **Parity whitespace.** A text node sits between every pair of adjacent inline elements that both
   carry text: the disclosure row's label and id, the register line's label and status, the schedule
   row, the relations and hub rows, the header's register and date, the hub breadcrumb and the hub
   member's name and question. The stylesheet's half-rem becomes a quarter-rem beside the space.
8. **Storage keys are never painted.** `looksLikePageKey` covers `K1:`–`K4:`, `COMBO:`, `PRODUCT:`,
   `HOLD:` and `IK:`; `buildBlockBody` drops such an identifier from every row and fact, and
   `anchor()` drops it from a citation — an ingest that recorded no register record id had written
   the page's own key there, and the anchor read "Drugs@FDA · COMBO:NAME:… · 2026-08-28".
9. **Every visible list caps at six rows.** `groupRevealedRows` splits any run longer than six into
   six rows and a counted remainder marked `disclosed`, which the template paints as a closed
   `<details>`; the same cap applies to the block's facts, the computed sections, the relations, the
   hubs, the sources and the "Other registers" rows. The seven jurisdiction lines and the
   controlled-substance schedule table are not capped: both are fixed lists that are the block's
   own answer.
10. **The provenance timeline** fires only on three or more dated events, sorted, and its question
    names the first and last event kinds ("How did X get from first publication in 1975 to first
    approval in 1999?"). Measured consequence: seed 8 falls from **226 pages to 2** and is
    discarded by the 40-page floor, so the corpus renders no timeline block. Two dated points are
    not a timeline and the seed says so.
11. **`never-dosed` is retired.** "Has X ever reached a person?" answered its own absence; the
    header's evidence line "No human study recorded" carries it once.
12. **One relation per pair.** `identity_relations_v6.py` keeps the most specific relation per
    directed pair by a fixed specificity order and writes `relations-v6.parquet` and
    `canonical-v6.ndjson` beside the v5 files: **1,022 directed pairs deduplicated** (256
    stereoisomer-of over form-of, 223 the same relation in two spellings, 114 over racemate-of, and
    the rest) and 6 relation rows dropped. Every consumer that renders — the page text, the loader,
    the blocks, the Tier 3 sections, the hubs, the duplicate check, the release build — reads v6.
13. **"This record holds N fields" is furniture**, in the render and on the page.
14. **Hub tables.** Every absence cell carries `data-furniture`, so the rendered duplicate check
    reads hub content and not shared absence. Measured on the build, that raised the hub-to-hub
    figure before it lowered it — removing text both hubs share raises their Jaccard — from
    1,472 pairs at v8 to **5,959**. The survivor rule of §13(13) then applied at rendered Jaccard
    ≥ 0.5 as §14(14) directs, over three passes, each re-measured on a restarted server:
    **923 hubs built → 714 published, 258 aliases**, and **1 hub-to-hub pair** left at 0.5703
    (`target/hdac8` ↔ `target/histone-deacetylase-1-histone-deacetylase-1`). Complete linkage will
    not fuse that pair into either page's group, which is the rule working as written, and it is
    recorded here rather than merged by a weaker linkage. `link_graph_check.py` passes all seven
    rules on the build: 710 published hubs, every one answering 200, 6,052 member links answering
    200.
15. **The trace classifier accepts a list.** A trace that is a semicolon-separated list of traces
    resolves when every element resolves and they are all of one class, so the grouped curated line
    (`frdb:ddi:16659; frdb:ddi:16660; …`) and the grouped label line's counterpart list are
    executed element by element rather than failing as unrecognised. Block rows were already exempt
    from both checks (`kind != 'sentence'`), and facts are now marked `fact` rather than `row` so a
    rule about what a reader meets without opening anything can be applied to them. The three
    one-value prose blocks draw 4 named are rows: the substituent comparison, the trial-size block
    (median, largest, counted, recorded status, posted results) and the mechanism block (action,
    target, mechanism-row count, what is not recorded), which keeps only the register's own quoted
    wording as prose.
16. **The self-audit** (`scripts/revamp/self_audit.py`, `data/revamp/self-audit-round4.json`):
    30 pages (10 per tier, seed 20260911) and 10 hubs rendered on this build, eleven mechanical
    rules checked against the painted DOM with the `dom_parity.py` extraction — **30/30 pages and
    10/10 hubs**, 3,321 individual rule applications, 0 failures. Three defects it found on the way
    were fixed: the hub breadcrumb and the hub member line joined two inline elements with no text
    node, and the audit's own supervision rule was reading the block's second paragraph (this
    record's study scope) as if it were the answer.

**The render.** `data/revamp/render-v9/{text,text-with-furniture,provenance}`, **28,657 pages**;
